# MPL_106+107 — research notes

## Scope, evidence, and reading conventions

These notes were written after the transcription was frozen at **2026-11-16 13:43:19 UTC**. The 41 scans were transcribed and visually audited by the principal reader in one stream, without OCR or transcription subagents; the subsequent parallel work concerned only research comparisons against the frozen text. No contextual or mathematical correction has been put back into the transcription.

`106/012` means archive cote 106, PDF scan 12, not manuscript page 12. Manuscript numbers are given separately where useful. Quotations preserve the frozen reading; a quotation containing `\unsure{…}` remains uncertain, and a mathematical reconstruction in these notes is not a replacement reading.

The evidence has four distinct levels: (1) the personally inspected scans and frozen diplomatic transcription; (2) the manuscript-text layer reproduced in the supplied *Les Dérivateurs* companion and *Pursuing Stacks* files, whose underlying scans were not newly inspected here; (3) the companion's interleaved `dcomment`/editorial material and earlier AI research returns; and (4) independently checked arguments or external primary literature. A supplied status such as **Proved**, **Known**, or **Refuted** is the return's claim unless its entry explicitly records an intake or review; these notes do not turn those statuses into independent verifications.

The contextual comparisons below use `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md` (abbreviated **RS**), `context/NEW_RESULTS.md`, and `context/DOSSIER_STATEMENTS_WITH_STATUS.md`. The latter has **255 statements**: 10 STRATA, 76 PATHS, 113 BISITE, and 56 RESIDUE; the complete concordance is included below, together with all 12 numbered NEW_RESULTS entries and its three trailing “known after all” notices. Exact source statements and statuses are retained in the concordance, so an absent result is not silently omitted.

The context-search records identify queries, files, and manuscript-page markers. Search locators in a companion file are textual locators, not new scan attestations. No historical priority or date of composition is inferred from a chapter number, an archive cote, a recycled computer listing, or the planning README.

## Reading guide

The itemized manuscript notes are **C106-01–20**, **RQ-01–14**, **O1–O4**, **R1–R11**, **H01–H13**, **SP-L01–07**, and **R106-V1**. Each keeps a short substantive note separate from provenance and, where needed, a longer verification appendix. Standing registers give the manuscript's own unverified steps and its title/count evidence. The 255-row dossier concordance and 15-entry NEW_RESULTS concordance are complete comparison registers, with supplied statements and statuses preserved.

| Section | Contents |
|---|---|
| A–B | Cote 106 itemized notes and post-freeze reading questions |
| C–D | Every explicit self-flag recorded; titles, counts, numbering, reused-stock dates |
| E–F | Main findings; conditional modern verification of the short-fraction and section claims |
| G | Relative Hom, copowers, external-product failures, and exact companion parallels |
| H | All 15 NEW_RESULTS records; sequential colimits, relative-square criterion, counterexamples, verification appendices, and primary references |
| I | Bounded first-appearance leads for strong W(7), total asphericity, and relative Theorem A |
| J | All 255 dossier statements, including exact supplied status/locator and per-row absence or relation |
| K | Completion, validation limits, and recovery-record guide |

The longer proofs in F and H are explicitly our conditional verifications or applications of known results; the short manuscript notes remain one to four sentences. Appendix letters in **H** are local to that section. The reference-comparison entries **C1–C11** in G are a source index used by O1–O4 and R1–R11, not a second set of manuscript claims.

## A. Cote 106: replacement, short fractions, and the unresolved section criterion

### C106-01 — The opening replacement theorem and the axiom boundary

**Location / kind:** 106/002, opening paragraph; unproved claim. **Context search:** CF-AXIOMS and CF-REPLACEMENT; `context/companion/chunks/DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.108–110, Théorème-Scholie 2(I); `context/ps_chapters_VI_VII/ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–532. **RS:** Hua, findings register, **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, conjecture candidate, intake “new to us”; no selected RS entry separately identifies the exact C1–C3 system.

The opening says “il est connu que” the localization of the cofibrant objects is equivalent to the localization of all objects, and then attempts to construct a quasi-inverse. The analogous fibrant-replacement assertion in VI ms.108–110 is a contextual counterpart, not an identification of hypotheses; the present leaf adds a concrete cofibrant, weak-lifting construction. Rădulescu-Banu (2009, Theorem 6.1.6) supplies a standard precise replacement theorem, but applying it literally requires spelling out the manuscript's abbreviated axioms.

### C106-02 — Independence of the lifted arrow is initially conditional

**Location / kind:** 106/002, after the first weak-lifting diagrams; self-flagged. **Context search:** CF-CANCELLATION and CF-REPLACEMENT; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.108–110, especially the distinction between a functorial replacement and one constructed after localization. **RS:** bears on Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, with the same unreviewed candidate status.

After “Je dis qu'il ne dépend pas du choix”, the argument reduces to cancellation of a weak equivalence on two maps between cofibrant objects and explicitly continues “Si ce lemme est valable”. This is a condition on the proposed quasi-inverse, rather than a proof of the cancellation lemma. The context has the dual replacement problem; these pages add the precise cancellation bottleneck and later formulate it as a criterion at 106/006.

### C106-03 — Identity and composition compatibility is asserted before that bottleneck is discharged

**Location / kind:** 106/002, last paragraph, and 106/004, upper diagrams; unproved claim. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION and CF-REPLACEMENT; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.77–82, path composition, and `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.108–110. **RS:** related Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; **CATHOT E.26**, Open (return's claim), `CATHOT_results.csv`, VII ms.108–123, concerns a different later comparison.

The sentence “la compatibilité … aux identités et aux compositions est immédiate” presupposes the choice independence just isolated. The diagrams on 106/004 exhibit how two successive lifts are to be compared, but no independent proof of the opening cancellation lemma is supplied there. This is a limited precursor to the all-type path-composition discussion in VII, not a settlement of CATHOT E.26.

### C106-04 — The naturality check is flagged and then actually carried out

**Location / kind:** 106/004, lower half; self-flagged, resolved locally. **Context search:** CF-REPLACEMENT and CF-CANCELLATION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.109–110, the naturality of replacement maps after localization. **RS:** related Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; no new selected RS theorem is proved.

The manuscript says “il faut vérifier que c'est fonctoriel” for the comparison from ΨΦ to the canonical localization functor and immediately computes the required equality using α_y = β_f i_f. Its “cqfd” closes this naturality calculation conditional on the already defined Φ; it does not close the earlier choice-independence question. The context restates the same formal role of natural replacement maps in the dual situation.

### C106-05 — The opening theorem is recast as a cancellation criterion

**Location / kind:** 106/002 and 106/006, “En résumé”; withdrawn in the same text / reformulation. **Context search:** CF-CANCELLATION and CF-REPLACEMENT; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.108–110. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness** is related but concerns the later, different section criterion.

The initial “il est connu” statement at 106/002 is followed at 106/006 by “il f. et s.” and the explicit cancellation condition for Ψ to be an equivalence. This is a change in the status of the attempted proof, not an explicit denial of the standard replacement theorem. The cancellation criterion for the inclusion of cofibrant objects must not be conflated with the section-independence criterion for short fractions at 106/016.

### C106-06 — Restriction to cofibrant objects and pushouts

**Location / kind:** 106/006, bracketed NB; unproved claim. **Context search:** CF-AXIOMS and CF-COUNIVERSAL; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.108–110, for the dual restriction, and `ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–532, for the need for pushouts in a cofibration structure. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, unreviewed candidate; thematic only to **PATHS D.22**, Open, `PATHS_results.csv`, intrinsic cofibrations without an initial object.

The NB says that the induced structure on C_cof again satisfies the abbreviated axioms and that the inclusion preserves the pushouts whose existence is postulated. Under the standard cofibration-category axioms this is the expected closure argument, but the manuscript does not enumerate it. These leaves explicitly assume an initial object at 106/002, so they do not answer the “without initial object” part of PATHS D.22.

### C106-07 — Passing from pushout composition to a category of components

**Location / kind:** 106/006–008; unproved claim. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, §7, ms.59–82, especially ms.77–82. **RS:** dossier **PATHS A.P1**, supplied Proved claim but not itself selected in RS; related **CATHOT E.26**, Open, VII ms.108–123.

Pushout composition is declared “associative à iso can. près”, and the induced composition on π₀ is declared “associative (str. !)”. The canonical associativity and unit isomorphisms are sufficient at the component level, provided the stated pushouts and comparison morphisms remain inside the roof categories. VII develops the more general all-type path formalism; this packet gives a short-roof version and no full mapping-space comparison.

### C106-08 — Couniversal weak equivalences are proposed as a broader input

**Location / kind:** 106/008, paragraph beginning “Pour l'instant”; unpursued implication. **Context search:** CF-COUNIVERSAL; `DER_XIIb-a.tex`, ms.111–115, the later couniversal/cofibration questions; `ps6.tex`, §125, pp.532–533, the programme based on W alone. **RS:** **TOTASPH F.27**, Open (return's claim), `TOTASPH_results.csv`, XII ms.111–114; **POLES H.5**, Proved (return's claim), `POLES_results.csv`, boundary for all sieves to be cofibrations.

The manuscript suggests retaining the construction when the chosen denominators are couniversal weak equivalences, without insisting that cof = cof_W. This addresses the stability needed to compose the particular roofs, not recognition of every couniversal morphism or closure of dwyerienne sieves under composition. It therefore adds a methodological link to the later open questions without settling them.

### C106-09 — The proposed inverse is obstructed by the cofibration condition

**Location / kind:** 106/008, inverse-search diagrams, and 106/010, first half; withdrawn in the same text. **Context search:** CF-CANCELLATION and CF-COUNIVERSAL; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.65–73, inversion of denominators using arbitrary-length paths. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; dossier **PATHS A.P1** (Proved claim, not selected in RS).

The inverse sought through “g?” and “i?” at 106/008 is announced at 106/010 as “l'inverse cherché”, then its proposed connecting diagram is rejected because αi = f need not be a cofibration. The proposed repair βf is also rejected: if cofibrations are monomorphisms and f is not monic, postcomposition cannot make βf monic. This refutes that particular repair, not the possibility of a different zigzag in the roof category or the standard short-fraction theorem.

### C106-10 — Surjectivity is proved separately from faithfulness

**Location / kind:** 106/010, final paragraph; unpursued implication. **Context search:** CF-CANCELLATION and CF-COMPOSITION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.68–73, localization by effects of paths. **RS:** dossier **PATHS A.P1** (Proved claim, not selected); related **CATHOT E.26**, Open, concerns stronger comparisons.

The calculation concluding “OK” shows that the roof (g,i) has image [f]⁻¹ in the localization, and hence establishes the claimed surjectivity on localized arrows using composition. It does not show that this roof is already an inverse inside the component category. The explicit separation between fullness and faithfulness is the useful contribution of these pages to the later path-localization comparison.

### C106-11 — Faithfulness remains explicitly open at the point of revision

**Location / kind:** 106/012, first paragraph; self-flagged. **Context search:** CF-CANCELLATION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.68–73 and ms.82, ordinary localization versus later diagram-category comparisons. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, conjecture candidate; related **CATHOT E.26**, Open, `CATHOT_results.csv`.

“Il n'est pas clair” refers specifically to faithfulness of the component-roof comparison, after bijectivity on objects and surjectivity on arrows. The manuscript relates it to inverting all of W in the roof construction, then changes the definition of that construction. It is therefore inaccurate to read the earlier surjectivity argument as a proof of full faithfulness or of a higher path-space theorem.

### C106-12 — The conjectured weakness of inverting only acyclic cofibrations

**Location / kind:** 106/012, middle; conjecture / expressed doubt. **Context search:** CF-COUNIVERSAL and CF-AXIOMS; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.109–112, fine versus coarse localizations, and `ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–533. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, unreviewed candidate; no exact selected RS statement settles this local question.

The text first says “semble-t-il” about W₀⁻¹C → W⁻¹C and then “Mais je doute” that inverting weak cofibrations suffices. The W₀ in this leaf is W ∩ cof; the fine localizer W₀ in VI has a different definition and must not be identified by notation alone. Under explicit standard cofibration hypotheses the doubt has an elementary positive resolution, recorded separately below as our argument.

### C106-13 — Two definitions of Ch₀ and C̃ must remain separate

**Location / kind:** 106/006 and 106/012; withdrawn in the same text / changed definition. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION and CF-AXIOMS; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.65–73, allowed backwards arrows; Horel (2016, Introduction). **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, candidate; dossier **PATHS A.P1**, Proved claim, not selected in RS.

“Je rectifie donc le tir” replaces roofs with arbitrary forward arrow and backwards acyclic cofibration by roofs whose forward arrow is a cofibration and whose backwards arrow is merely in W. The same symbols Ch₀ and C̃ are reused, so conclusions before and after 106/012 require a comparison and cannot be transferred by notation alone. The standard short-roof literature is a relevant comparison, but the equivalence of these two component presentations is additional reasoning, supplied with explicit hypotheses in the literature-review notes below.

### C106-15 — Choice independence is the outstanding construction problem

**Location / kind:** 106/014, middle and lower paragraphs; self-flagged. **Context search:** CF-SECTION and CF-CANCELLATION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.109–110, ad hoc construction after localization; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.82, anticipated comparison theorems. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, candidate.

For the roof obtained by factorization and weak lifting, the text says “il faut montrer que ça ne dépend pas des choix faits” and then “il faut voir qu'ils sont dans une = composante connexe”. A common third factorization is proposed at 106/014 and constructed at 106/016; the remaining issue is independence of the chosen section for one factorization. These leaves thus sharpen the problem rather than merely repeating the earlier general faithfulness question.

### C106-16 — The common refinement and its retained section

**Location / kind:** 106/016, first diagram and following paragraph; unproved claim with construction. **Context search:** CF-SECTION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.104–110, replacement and common inverses. **RS:** related Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; no separate selected RS result.

The coproduct-pushout-factorization diagram supplies the promised common third factorization, and the text says “il est trivial” that a section is inherited if either earlier weak equivalence has one. The section assertion follows by composing a section with the comparison map into the common refinement, but it does not identify the two sections thus obtained. This is exactly why the manuscript next isolates section independence rather than declaring the construction finished.

### C106-17 — The terminal criterion is a conjectural sufficiency statement on the page

**Location / kind:** 106/016, condition (*) and final paragraph; self-flagged / expectation. **Context search:** CF-SECTION and CF-CANCELLATION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.107–110, equality of two inverses after localization; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.68–73. **RS:** Hua, findings register, **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, conjecture candidate, intake “new to us”.

Condition (*) asks that the component of the roof (1_x,i) be independent of a section i of f ∈ W. The final words, “comme on doit pouvoir vérifier aisément, en construisant bel et bien le foncteur cherché”, leave the sufficiency proof unwritten; necessity follows from faithfulness because both sections become f⁻¹ after localization. Hua's entry captures the subject of the criterion, but its prose must not be read as evidence that this packet supplies the missing verification.

### C106-18 — Our conditional resolution of the W₀-localization doubt

**Location / kind:** response to 106/012; own idea, verification of a standard consequence. **Confidence:** high under the stated standard hypotheses; the remaining uncertainty is their identification with the manuscript's abbreviated C1–C3. **Context search:** CF-AXIOMS, CF-COUNIVERSAL, CF-SECTION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.107–112, related but different localizations. **RS:** bears on Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; no exact selected RS result is being re-proved.

Assume every object is cofibrant, coproduct inclusions are cofibrations, and every map factors as a cofibration followed by a weak equivalence: for f:X→Y in W, factor (f,1):X⊔Y→Y as X⊔Y→Z→Y, with components j_X,j_Y and retraction r. Both j_X and j_Y are acyclic cofibrations by two-out-of-three, so any functor inverting W ∩ cof sends r to F(j_Y)⁻¹ and f=rj_X to an isomorphism. Hence W₀⁻¹C→W⁻¹C is an equivalence under these hypotheses, without needing to assume the disputed faithfulness of the particular roof category; this is a standard Brown-factorization consequence, not a novelty claim (Brown, 1973, p.421, dual factorization; Rădulescu-Banu, 2009, §1.3).

### C106-19 — Higher mapping data remain an additional problem

**Location / kind:** 106/006–016; unpursued implication. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.82 and commentary following ms.87–91, ordinary π₀ versus mapping types. **RS:** dossier **PATHS A.P1**, Proved claim; **CATHOT E.26**, Open, VII ms.108–123; these statuses are not reconciled by the packet.

The construction in this packet uses connected components of roof categories, so even a successful faithfulness proof would compute ordinary localized Hom sets only. The nerve of a roof category, its comparison with a hammock localization, and its behaviour under diagram formation require additional theorems of the kind discussed by Horel (2016). This distinction prevents a short-fraction calculation here from being treated as a proof of every PATHS mapping-space claim.

### C106-20 — The W-only programme is a genuine thematic link, not a dating tool

**Location / kind:** 106/008 and 106/012; unpursued implication. **Context search:** CF-AXIOMS and CF-COUNIVERSAL; `ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–533, dated “13.10.” in the supplied text. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; no selected RS entry fixes the date of these leaves.

The willingness to vary cofibrations while retaining W parallels the *Pursuing Stacks* passage proposing weak equivalences as the basic data and a derivator as the model-independent structure. This contextual link helps explain the mathematical motivation but neither identifies “Brown” with “Baues” nor dates these undated sheets to the dated PS entry. It also supplies no relative Theorem A statement on these pages.

## B. Reading questions raised after freezing

### RQ-01 — Brown in the ink, Baues in the comparison register

**Location / kind:** 106/002, first sentence; reading question. **Context search:** CF-AXIOMS; `ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–532, explicitly Baues; RS Hua entry explicitly Baues. **RS:** **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**, candidate, intake “new to us”.

The frozen reading is underlined “Brown”, with doubtful “C1 . C3|C1 : C3” and “pour mé. C0”, whereas Hua's register calls the assumptions Baues's. The PS context provides a reason to investigate that attribution, not permission to substitute it for the observed name. The exact axiom source remains a reading and bibliographic question; Brown's published fibrant axioms are labelled A–E (1973, pp.420–421), which does not resolve the manuscript numbering.

### RQ-02 — The composition formula at the first roof construction

**Location / kind:** 106/006, final display; reading question. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.77–82. **RS:** related Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**; dossier **PATHS A.P1**, Proved claim.

The frozen formula retains `\unsure{g'g|g'f}` rather than silently choosing the composition suggested by the diagram. The contextual composition formalism makes g′f mathematically natural, but it does not decide the written second letter. This uncertainty concerns the local formula, not the existence of the pushout construction.

### RQ-03 — Identity subscript in the canonical arrow-to-roof map

**Location / kind:** 106/008, f↦(f,Id) display; reading question. **Context search:** CF-COMPOSITION; `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.68–73. **RS:** related Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**.

The frozen reading `\operatorname{Id}_{\unsure{x|y}}` preserves the unresolved subscript. For a roof x→y←y the definition suggests Id_y, but that type check belongs here and cannot settle the ink after the freeze. The later path-localization context has identity insertions but no independent witness to this specific subscript.

### RQ-04 — The direction of β and the role of its section

**Location / kind:** 106/014, factorization diagram and “β a une section i”; reading question. **Context search:** CF-SECTION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.104–110. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**.

The drawn β is retained in the same visible direction as the section arrow although the prose calls i its section. A mathematical reconstruction would orient the retraction oppositely, as in standard weak-lifting factorization, but the frozen diagram is unchanged. This is a substantive diagram/prose tension and one of the lower-confidence parts of the packet.

### RQ-05 — Section primes and an occurrence of f rather than f₀

**Location / kind:** 106/016, middle paragraph and the factorization of [f₀,i]; reading question. **Context search:** CF-SECTION; `DER_VI-b.tex`, ms.107–110. **RS:** Hua **106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness**.

The section-prime readings remain explicitly doubtful, and the displayed decomposition retains `(f,Id)` where the intended typed composition appears to require `(f₀,Id)`. Common-refinement arguments in the context explain the intended roles of the maps but do not supply new evidence for the marks on this sheet. Neither potential correction has been introduced into the transcription.

### RQ-06 — Projection subscripts are deliberately cross-labelled

**Location / kind:** 107/004–016, product projections; reading question. **Context search:** HOM-RELATIVE and HOM-TENSOR; `DER_XVIII-a.tex`, ms.63–64. **RS:** dossier **BISITE E.3** and **BISITE F.1/F.4/F.7/F.8**, thematic ordinary precursors; their exact register status is retained in the concordance.

The frozen diagrams use p_B for the projection to A and p_A for the projection to B. Modern conventions or the later companion may invite the opposite naming, but the repeated cross-labelling is retained and used consistently when interpreting the formulas here. Any comparison that silently renames the projections risks changing the meanings of the two relative Hom functors.

### RQ-07 — Two readings of continuity must not be merged

**Location / kind:** 107/014 and 107/017, exclamation marks and duality notation; reading question. **Context search:** HOM-CONTINUITY and HOM-RELATIVE; `DER_XVIII-a.tex`, ms.63–64, original text and separate commentary. **RS:** thematic to **BISITE E.3** and the BISITE F ordinary/enhanced distinctions; no exact selected RS claim supplies a corrected reading.

The page uses `!`, `!!`, superscripts and subscripts, with explicit declarations of what is meant at particular points; these are not interchangeable primes. The later separate-continuity discussion exposes a mathematical distinction between joint and separate continuity, but that distinction cannot be used to normalize the manuscript's notation. The doubtful superscript on the deleted boxed formula at 107/017 remains doubtful.

### RQ-08 — Product symbol in the external-product definition

**Location / kind:** 107/014, definition of L⊠M; reading question. **Context search:** HOM-TENSOR and HOM-CONTINUITY; `DER_XVIII-a.tex`, ms.63–64. **RS:** thematic **BISITE E.3** and **BISITE F.1**; no selected RS statement adjudicates this symbol.

The frozen definition retains an external-product symbol between the two pulled-back factors, where an internal cartesian product is the type-correct candidate. The finite-set tests below examine the evident mathematical interpretation, not an amended transcription of the symbol. The later XVIII discussion is a conceptual parallel, not a second image of this formula.

### RQ-09 — The overwritten codomain does not license a blanket rejection of Yoneda

**Location / kind:** 107/014, fully faithful assertion and proof; reading question. **Context search:** HOM-CONTINUITY; `DER_XVIII-a.tex`, ms.63–64. **RS:** thematic BISITE E–F entries as recorded in the concordance.

The codomain is overwritten toward the presheaf category on Â×B̂, so the assertion must be evaluated with that overwritten text visible. Yoneda on the product category is fully faithful; the false step is the identification of its representable Hom-pair presheaf with the separately extended external-product evaluation. The notes' counterexample targets that identification rather than treating every fully faithful phrase in the passage as the same assertion.

### RQ-10 — The boundary exponent display is schematic and unfinished

**Location / kind:** 107/015, upper formulas and arrow marked “??”; reading question. **Context search:** HOM-TENSOR and HOT-RECTIFY; `DER_XIII-a.tex`, ms.48–50, and `DER_XIII-b.tex`, ms.63–65, ordinary limits, fibrant diagrams, and the corresponding commentary; dossier BISITE presheaf-model entries. **RS:** **BISITE C.1**, Proved (return's claim), `BISITE_results.csv`, VI ms.83 and XII ms.224–225, only a thematic connection.

The display for `(X^{\dot\Delta_n})_0` records a limit of the indicated face data and does not fully specify its indexing diagram; the following Hom(M,a)⇒a arrow carries two question marks. A matching-object interpretation is a possible mathematical explanation, but it does not justify supplying missing indexing arrows or a finished formula. The page remains an exploratory calculation, not a proved general matching-object theorem.

### RQ-11 — Quillen's proposition and the unresolved page numeral

**Location / kind:** 107/021, Quillen reference; reading question. **Context search:** HOT-RECTIFY; `ps6.tex`, §125, and the Quillen material identified in the literature section. **RS:** Hua **107-relative-square-rectification**, conjecture, intake “new to us”.

The frozen citation keeps `Ch. \unsure{II|I}`, proposition 4, and page `\unsure{2.4|2.9}`. The content is consistent with the simplicial homotopy lifting proposition in Quillen's *Homotopical algebra*, but the original printed page number was not independently verified in this run. A modern re-typesetting is identified as such and does not resolve the palaeographic alternatives.

### RQ-12 — Bars on the homotopy-class Hom comparison

**Location / kind:** 107/019, 107/021, and 107/025; reading question. **Context search:** HOT-LIMIT; `DER_III-V.tex`, V ms.44–46, invariant ind-objects, and `DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.82, diagram localizations. **RS:** Hua **107-hot-sequential-colimits**, literature claim; **CATHOT E.17**, Open, `CATHOT_results.csv`, a different variance of sequential question.

The manuscript explicitly introduces the homotopy-class interpretation at 107/021, while later displays sometimes write Hom without the bar. The notes interpret the return to “our initial pb” at 107/025 in that homotopy-category setting, while leaving every bar exactly as transcribed. Literal sets of maps and their π₀ have different limit behaviour; silently inserting bars would hide the very issue under investigation.

### RQ-13 — The last homotopy-extension diagram has uncertain indices and headless strokes

**Location / kind:** 107/025, middle diagrams; reading question. **Context search:** HOT-RECTIFY and HOT-LIMIT; `context/companion/chunks/DER_VII-b.tex`, ms.108–117, and `context/companion/chunks/DER_VII-c.tex`, ms.118–123, contextual coherent-comparison problems. **RS:** Hua **107-hot-sequential-colimits** and **107-relative-square-rectification**; **CATHOT E.26**, Open, remains a distinct question.

The frozen diagram retains `\unsure{i-1|i+1}` at several positions and preserves strokes with no visible arrowhead. The attempted induction suggests i+1, but that is not a reason to replace the first ink reading or to add heads. ### RQ-14 — Reused computer listings are independent textual evidence

The strongest findings of the comparison are the unfinished faithfulness argument in 106, the false unrestricted product-Hom formulas in 107, and the explicit surjectivity/injectivity split in the final sequential-colimit inquiry. Their detailed notes and independently checked counterexamples follow. The full result concordances distinguish direct partial forms, thematic precursors, and absence; a shared word such as “filtered”, “Hom”, or “cofibration” is never sufficient to identify two statements.

Strong W(7), total-aspherical ⇒ filtered, and relative Theorem A are **absent from the frozen 106+107 text**. The bounded first-appearance leads supplied by the other textual sources are stated in the STRATA/PATHS comparison below, with manuscript and commentary layers kept separate. They do not establish first composition or priority across the archive.

## F. Conditional verification of the cote 106 fraction and section claims

### Bounded conclusion

**Under the standard Brown cofibrant-object axioms, the modified category of roofs in 106/012–016 has the correct localized Hom-set after taking connected components; in particular, its section-independence criterion is automatic.** The same conclusion holds for cofibrant objects of a CF1–CF4 precofibration category, and the original roof convention of 106/006 also works under these hypotheses. This does **not** identify the manuscript’s incompletely specified “C1–C3” with a modern axiom list, complete its written proof, or establish novelty.

#### Own-idea verification note R106-V1

**Kind:** own idea / conditional verification of a standard consequence. **Location:** 106/012–016, especially the section criterion at 106/016. **Context:** PATHS A.P1, exact supplied status `claim (return: Proved)`, locator `VII ms.65–74`, in `context/DOSSIER_STATEMENTS_WITH_STATUS.md`; that ID is not selected in RESULTS_STATE. **Selected state lead:** Hua, findings register, `106-section-criterion-span-faithfulness`, status `conjecture (candidate: their search found no reference) | L…`, reported at `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, line 120, for package scans 010–016; this lead is not a reviewed theorem.

Under explicit CF1–CF4 hypotheses, joint-map cofibrant refinements identify the connected components of the modified strict-roof category with localized Hom-sets. Section independence follows because both section roofs map to one unrestricted roof, and this strict zigzag lifts through those refinements. **Confidence is high for that conditional deduction**, because the finite constructions use only stated factorization, pushout, and two-out-of-three axioms, with the localization universal property checked below. Identification of these sufficient hypotheses with the folder’s literal, incompletely specified C1–C3 remains unestablished, so this verification adds neither an unconditional manuscript theorem nor a novelty claim.

The argument below is the verification appendix for R106-V1.

### 1. What is actually being compared

Fix cofibrant objects \(x,y\), and write a roof as \(x\xrightarrow{a}z\xleftarrow{s}y\); its localization value is \([s]^{-1}[a]\). Morphisms of roofs are **strictly commuting maps of the middle object**, fixing \(x,y\), and connected components mean equivalence under finite zigzags of such morphisms.

| Roof category | Conditions | Location or role |
|---|---|---|
| \(F_0(x,y)\) | \(a\) arbitrary; \(s\in W\cap\mathrm{Cof}\) | Original construction, 106/006; doubt and revision at 106/012 |
| \(F_1(x,y)\) | \(a\in\mathrm{Cof}\); \(s\in W\) | Modified construction, 106/012–016 |
| \(R(x,y)\) | \(a\) arbitrary; \(s\in W\); middle object cofibrant | Auxiliary comparison category only |
| \(J(x,y)\) | \((a,s):x\amalg y\to z\) a cofibration; \(s\in W\) | Auxiliary full subcategory of both \(F_0,F_1\) |

For a middle map \(u:z\to z'\), the equality \(us=s'\) and two-out-of-three force \(u\in W\). Thus imposing “middle maps weak equivalences” adds no restriction to these strictly commuting roof morphisms; this does not say that arbitrary vertical arrows in an unrestricted all-type path construction are automatically weak equivalences.

### 2. Exact literature scope

Rădulescu-Banu’s Theorem 6.4.4 describes localized maps by weak-equivalence denominators and equality by common refinements **up to homotopy**. Theorem 6.4.5 permits numerators and joint maps that are cofibrations, with acyclic-cofibration denominators; its equality criterion still retains a homotopy condition on the numerators (2009, §6.4, pp.77–78). Consequently, those theorem statements should not be paraphrased as already asserting a strictly commuting common refinement of arbitrary roofs.

Horel describes precisely \(F_0\) in his introduction and reports the stronger assertion that its nerve models a localization mapping space; he also discusses composition by pushout (2016, pp.1–2). His introduction points to Cisinski for the version without functorial factorization, whereas Horel’s own partial-Brown-category definition imposes a functorial factorization condition (Definition 2.2 and Remark 2.3). The elementary \(\pi_0\) argument below needs no functorial choices and makes no corresponding nerve-equivalence assertion for \(F_1\).

### 3. Explicit sufficient hypotheses

Work in the cofibrant objects of a CF1–CF4 precofibration category: weak equivalences contain isomorphisms and satisfy two-out-of-three; cofibrations contain isomorphisms and are composition-stable; pushouts along cofibrations exist and preserve cofibrations, including acyclic ones; maps with cofibrant source factor as a cofibration followed by a weak equivalence. Finite coproducts of cofibrant objects are available. This is the standard finite hypothesis set related to Brown’s cofibrant-object axioms by Rădulescu-Banu (2009, Definition 1.1.1 and Proposition 2.1.2); CF5–CF6 and saturation are not used here.

### 4. A strict refinement argument comparing the three roof conventions

Set \(K=x\amalg y\). For an object \(r:K\to z\) of \(R\), factor it as

\[
K\xrightarrow[\mathrm{cof}]{j}Q
 \xrightarrow[\sim]{q}z.
\]

The \(y\)-leg of \(Q\) is a cofibration and is weak by two-out-of-three, so \(Q\in J\), and \(q\) is a strict roof morphism. This replacement stays inside \(F_0\) or \(F_1\) when its target roof belongs to that category.

For a strict roof morphism \(u:z\to z'\), choose such replacements \(Q\to z\) and \(Q'\to z'\). Form \(P=Q\amalg_KQ'\), map \(P\to z'\) using \(uq\) and \(q'\), and factor this map as \(P\hookrightarrow T\xrightarrow{\sim}z'\). Then \(T\in J\), and the induced strict roof morphisms \(Q\to T\leftarrow Q'\) are weak equivalences by two-out-of-three.

Every object is therefore reached from \(J\), and every arrow in a finite connecting zigzag can be replaced by a zigzag in \(J\). It follows directly, without assuming functorial replacements, that all the inclusions induce bijections

\[
\pi_0J\ \cong\ \pi_0F_0\ \cong\ \pi_0R
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\pi_0J\ \cong\ \pi_0F_1\ \cong\ \pi_0R.
\]

#### Why the common component set is the localized Hom-set

For completeness, the ordinary localization conclusion can also be checked without promoting Horel’s stronger mapping-space statement to unlisted hypotheses. Form a category \(H\) with the objects of \(C\) and Hom-sets \(\pi_0F_0(x,y)\); compose roofs by pushout along the backward acyclic cofibration, as in 106/006–008. This is well-defined on components, associative there, and receives \(C\to H\) by \(a\mapsto(a,1)\).

For \(w:x\to y\) in \(W\), factor \((1_y,w):y\amalg x\to y\) as a cofibration \((a,b):y\amalg x\to Q\), followed by \(q:Q\overset{\sim}{\to}y\). Both \(a,b\) are acyclic cofibrations, with \(qa=1_y\) and \(qb=w\), and the roof \((a,b):y\to x\) represents an inverse to \(w\). Indeed, one composite is the roof \((aw,b)\), connected in \(R(x,x)\) to \((w,w)\) by \(q\), while \((1_x,1_x)\to(w,w)\) is induced by \(w\); the other composite has middle object \(Q\amalg_x y\) and collapses to the identity roof using \(q\) and \(1_y\).

The comparison in the preceding paragraph transfers the first connection from \(R\) to \(F_0\). Every functor \(C\to D\) inverting \(W\) then factors uniquely through \(H\), with a roof sent to the inverse of the image of its denominator composed with the image of its numerator. Thus \(H\cong C[W^{-1}]\), and the canonical map

\[
\pi_0F_1(x,y)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Hom}_{C[W^{-1}]}(x,y)
\]

is bijective, in particular injective. This is a \(\pi_0\)-level verification; it does not establish a weak equivalence between the **nerves** of \(F_0,F_1,R\).

### 5. The manuscript’s section criterion

Let \(w:x\to y\) lie in \(W\), with sections \(i,i':y\to x\). Two-out-of-three makes both sections weak equivalences, so \((1_x,i)\) and \((1_x,i')\) are objects of \(F_1(x,y)\), and their localization values are both \([w]\). The bijection above proves that their connected components agree.

There is also a direct strict-roof witness before using the localization theorem: both roofs map in \(R\) to \((w,1_y)\), by the middle map \(w\). Replacing this short zigzag through the construction of §4 gives a zigzag entirely in \(F_1\), proving exactly the section independence isolated at 106/016. The unmodified roof convention therefore was not invalid under the standard axioms merely because the proposed elementary inverse argument had stalled at 106/010–012.

### 6. Manuscript, Hua, and the Pursuing Stacks context

The frozen 106/002 text names **Brown**, while 106/006 reduces to the case where every object is cofibrant; the axioms labelled C1–C3 are not fully reproduced in this folder. Hua’s findings-register description names **Baues** and compresses the outcome into faithfulness, whereas 106/012–016 records a revision and a final criterion whose verification the author proposes. The safe report is therefore a **standard-theory conditional resolution of the manuscript’s residual question**, not an unconditional theorem under an independently verified literal C1–C3 list and not a novelty claim.

The supplied *Pursuing Stacks* text, `context/ps_chapters_VI_VII/ps6.tex`, §125, pp.530–532 (section headed 13.10), explicitly discusses Baues’s cofibration/fibration categories and then a programme starting from weak equivalences alone. This makes Baues a relevant contextual reading lead but does not override the word Brown at 106/002 or identify the two sets of hypotheses. The principal reader’s separate verification of Brown’s original Proposition 2 (p.424) concerns the fibrant-object, right-fraction calculus in \(\pi C\); dualization supplies a comparison, not evidence that this folder states the same axiom list verbatim.

The primary PDF has a December 1973 volume/title page and a 1974 copyright; Rădulescu-Banu’s bibliography key is `[Bro74]`, while Horel uses `[Bro73]`, so the dating convention must not be silently treated as a second paper.

The proof in §§3–5 is an explicit deduction under the stated axioms, independent of the unreviewed companion runs.

## G. Cote 107: relative Hom, external products, and contextual parallels

### Own audit observations: ordinary external products

These observations test literal formulas in the frozen text and do not change that text. The finite-set calculations are elementary checks made for this mapping, with no novelty claim.

#### O1 — 107/014

**Kind:** own idea / audit observation; elementary counterexample or continuity test. **Confidence:** high; elementary finite-set check.

The overwritten codomain on 107/014 should not be confused with the genuine Yoneda embedding of $\widehat A\times\widehat B$: the problematic step identifies the extension $X(L^{\prime},M^{\prime})=\operatorname{Hom}(L^{\prime}\boxtimes M^{\prime},X)$ at $X=L\boxtimes M$ with the representable functor $\operatorname{Hom}(L^{\prime},L)\times\operatorname{Hom}(M^{\prime},M)$. Take $A=B=e$ and all four presheaves to be the two-element set: the first set has $4^4=256$ elements and the second has $2^2\cdot2^2=16$, so the claimed identification is false. This is an elementary audit calculation made here, not a claim of novelty.

**Status connection:** No selected RESULTS_STATE entry states this ordinary claim; related BISITE E.3 and F.7 are absent from that selected state.

**Context-search relations:** C3 below.

#### O2 — 107/014

**Kind:** own idea / audit observation; elementary counterexample or continuity test. **Confidence:** high; empty-limit test.

The proof says that both sides transform colimits to limits separately, but the purported representable product fails that condition: with $L^{\prime}=\varnothing$ it is $1\times\operatorname{Hom}(M^{\prime},M)$, which need not be terminal, whereas $\operatorname{Hom}(\varnothing\boxtimes M^{\prime},L\boxtimes M)=1$. The failure is already preservation of the empty limit in one contravariant variable, and the four nonempty finite sets of O1 give a separate numerical contradiction to the claimed identification. The original-text parallel in the supplied companion is XVIII ms63–64, Lemma4.2.3 and its following objection; its AI commentary is not needed for this verification.

**Status connection:** No selected RESULTS_STATE entry states this ordinary claim; related BISITE E.3 and F.7 are absent from that selected state.

**Context-search relations:** C3 below.

#### O3 — 107/016

**Kind:** own idea / audit observation; elementary counterexample or continuity test. **Confidence:** high; separates valid corollary and false specialization.

The first assertion that $p_B^*$ preserves internal Hom is the ordinary projection inverse-image formula and must be kept separate from the subsequent mixed formula involving arbitrary $M^{\prime}$. The latter yields the universally asserted $L^M=L$, which is false already for $A=B=e$ and $L=M=2$, because $|2^2|=4$ whereas $|2|=2$. Thus the useful ordinary projection comparison does not validate the mixed external-Hom claim or any derived projection, exchange, or duality statement.

**Status connection:** No selected RESULTS_STATE entry states this ordinary claim; related BISITE E.3 and F.7 are absent from that selected state.

**Context-search relations:** C3, C10 below.

#### O4 — 107/014

**Kind:** own idea / audit observation; elementary counterexample or continuity test. **Confidence:** high; separate versus joint continuity check.

The marginal identification of separate continuity ($!!$) with joint continuity ($!$) on $(\widehat A\times\widehat B)^o$ is a distinct failure. For $A=B=e$, the bifunctor $F(L,M)=\operatorname{Hom}(L\times M,2)$ carries colimits to limits in each variable separately, but the coproduct of $(1,0)$ and $(0,1)$ in $\mathrm{Set}\times\mathrm{Set}$ is $(1,1)$, and $F(1,1)=2$ whereas $F(1,0)\times F(0,1)=1$. Conversely, the genuine represented Hom of the product category is jointly continuous, but its product formula fails separate empty-limit preservation as O2 shows. Thus neither continuity condition may be substituted for the other in this argument.

**Status connection:** No selected RESULTS_STATE entry states this ordinary claim; related BISITE E.3 and F.7 are absent from that selected state.

**Context-search relations:** C3 below.

### 2. Short research notes

#### H01 — The requested universal property is an ordinary categorical one

- **Kind:** Self-flagged question; negative answer verified here for the ordinary weak-homotopy localizer.
- **Context search:** `context/companion/chunks/DER_III-V.tex`, V ms. **43–49**: **adds** the explicit ordinary-Hot universal property to the companion’s questions about invariant ind-objects and unions; `context/ps_chapters_VI_VII/ps7.tex`, §136, ms. **566–567**: **differs** from the derived homotopy-colimit construction.
- **RESULTS_STATE:** **CATHOT E.17 [Open (the return’s claim)]**, `CATHOT_results.csv`, is related but has different invariant-ind-object hypotheses; its status is unchanged. The exact ordinary-colimit question appears as **Hua `107-hot-sequential-colimits` [first-pass claim to check]** in `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, not as a settled register result.

**107/019, manuscript p. 18, circled a:** “Filtering \(\varinjlim\) exist in \((\mathrm{Hot})\)?” is made precise by the bijection \([X_\infty,Y]\to\varprojlim_n[X_n,Y]\), so existence of a homotopy colimit alone does not answer it. For the ordinary weak-homotopy localizer \(W_\infty\), the finite-telescope example in Appendix A refutes this proposed universal property even when all transition maps are cofibrations. The stronger nonexistence statement in Appendix B answers the opening existence question negatively for that sequence; it makes no assertion about every possible \(W\)-localization.

#### H02 — The surjectivity argument has the right hypothesis

- **Kind:** Unproved claim or abbreviated manuscript argument with an incomplete general rectification argument; the special-case surjectivity argument is completed and verified here.
- **Context search:** `context/companion/chunks/DER_III-V.tex`, V ms. **48**: **restates** the need to replace homotopy-commuting maps by strict transitions, while **adding** the explicit cofibration/fibrant-target mechanism; `context/companion/chunks/DER_XIII-b.tex`, XIII ms. **73–74**: **differs** from preservation of weak equivalences between fibrant inverse towers.
- **RESULTS_STATE:** No separate surjectivity theorem is selected; **CATHOT E.17 [Open (the return’s claim)]**, `CATHOT_results.csv`, supplies related context only. **Hua `107-hot-sequential-colimits` [first-pass claim to check]**, `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, asserts the manuscript proves surjectivity; our checked model-category completion supports that assertion.

**107/021, manuscript p. 20, circled b; 107/023, manuscript p. 22; 107/025, manuscript p. 24, d:** the text replaces the target by a Kan object, adjusts representatives recursively, and concludes “surjective,” emphasizing cofibration transition maps. For a sequence of cofibrant objects with cofibration transitions in a simplicial model category and a fibrant target, the restriction maps of mapping spaces form a tower of Kan fibrations, and lifting paths successively proves precisely this surjectivity (Hovey, 1999, Lemma 4.2.2; Hirschhorn, 2015, Theorem 2.1). This does not assert uniqueness of the compatible choices.

#### H03 — The missing injectivity is a genuine phantom-map defect

- **Kind:** Own idea / application of a known counterexample to the manuscript’s self-flagged injectivity gap; not a new counterexample or priority claim.
- **Context search:** `context/companion/chunks/DER_III-V.tex`, V ms. **48–49**: **adds** a phantom-map obstruction to the stated stagewise/global homotopy question; `context/companion/chunks/DER_XIII-b.tex`, XIII ms. **73–74**, commentary: **differs** from its inverse-limit complex with the same completion quotient.
- **RESULTS_STATE:** **CATHOT D.6 [Open (the return’s claim)]** and **CATHOT E.17 [Open (the return’s claim)]**, `CATHOT_results.csv`: related questions only, unchanged because the present target is K(Z,2) and the hypotheses differ. **Hua `107-hot-sequential-colimits` [first-pass claim to check]**, `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, correctly points to a lim¹ obstruction.
- **Confidence:** **High**: Hatcher explicitly computes the telescope cohomology; Appendix A independently checks the Ext quotient, nonzero maps, and literal simplicial-presheaf realization.

**107/025:** “\(f_i\sim f'_i\quad\forall i\)” is exactly the insufficient hypothesis exposed by taking finite subtelescopes \(T_n\) of \(S^1\xrightarrow{p}S^1\xrightarrow{p}\cdots\) and target \(K(\mathbb Z,2)\). Every \([T_n,K(\mathbb Z,2)]\) vanishes, whereas \([T,K(\mathbb Z,2)]\cong\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z\ne0\); Hatcher (2002, Example 3F.9, p. 315) explicitly computes this telescope's cohomology, and Milnor (1962, Lemma 2, p. 338) supplies the exact sequence. Appendix A verifies that the example lies literally in the manuscript's \(A=\Delta\) monomorphism setting.

#### H04 — There need not be any ordinary colimit, even a different one

- **Kind:** Own idea / verified implication of standard tools answering the manuscript’s existence question; not stated in the manuscript and not a novelty claim.
- **Context search:** `context/companion/chunks/DER_III-V.tex`, V ms. **46–49**: **adds** nonexistence of any ordinary colimit for one specific sequence, beyond failure of a chosen union to have the desired property; `context/ps_chapters_VI_VII/ps7.tex`, §136, ms. **566–567**: **differs** from the existence of derived homotopy colimits.
- **RESULTS_STATE:** No selected entry states this nonexistence theorem; **CATHOT E.17 [Open (the return’s claim)]**, `CATHOT_results.csv`, concerns a distinct invariant-ind-object question and remains unchanged. The opening existence question is part of **Hua `107-hot-sequential-colimits` [first-pass claim to check]**, `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`.
- **Confidence:** **High**: Appendix B checks naturality in every abelian group, the unbased convention, Yoneda, and the universal-coefficient contradiction; no finite-generation assumption is used.

**107/019:** for this same degree-\(p\) circle sequence there is **no ordinary colimit object in unbased Hot**, not merely a failure of its canonical telescope cocone. If such an object \(L\) existed, testing Eilenberg–MacLane targets would force \(H_1L\cong\mathbb Z[1/p]\) and \(H^2(L;\mathbb Z)=0\), contradicting the universal-coefficient injection \(\operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathbb Z[1/p],\mathbb Z)\hookrightarrow H^2(L;\mathbb Z)\). This is our verified implication of standard tools, not an assertion in the manuscript and not a novelty claim; Appendix B checks naturality and the unbased convention.

### Appendix A. Verification of the telescope counterexample

This is a standard example, not a novelty claim. Fix a prime \(p\), let \(f_n:S^1\to S^1\) have degree \(p\), and form its mapping telescope \(T\). Let \(T_n\) be the finite telescope ending at its \(n\)-th circle, with \(T_0=S^1\). Each \(T_n\) is a finite CW complex deforming onto that terminal circle, each \(T_n\hookrightarrow T_{n+1}\) is an inclusion of subcomplexes, and the induced bonding map on \(H_1\) is multiplication by \(p\). Thus

\[
H_1(T;\mathbb Z)=\mathop{\rm colim}(\mathbb Z\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb Z\xrightarrow{p}\cdots)=\mathbb Z[1/p],
\qquad H_2(T;\mathbb Z)=0.
\]

The universal-coefficient sequence, or Milnor's sequence applied to the finite subtelescopes, gives

\[
H^2(T;\mathbb Z)
\cong\operatorname{Ext}^1_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z[1/p],\mathbb Z)
\cong\varprojlim{}^1(\mathbb Z\xleftarrow{p}\mathbb Z\xleftarrow{p}\cdots)
\cong\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z.
\]

Milnor (1962, Lemmas 1–2, pp. 337–339) verifies the limit and exact-sequence tools; Hatcher (2002, Examples 3F.3, 3F.7, 3F.9 and the following Ext calculation, pp. 312–316) explicitly treats this telescope. Here is a self-contained verification of the algebraic term, to fix the convention and exclude confusing \(\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z\) with a different completion quotient. A free resolution has differential

\[
\bigoplus_{n\ge0}\mathbb Ze_n\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\ge0}\mathbb Ze_n,
\qquad e_n\longmapsto e_n-pe_{n+1},
\]

with cokernel \(\mathbb Z[1/p]\), by sending the target basis vector \(e_n\) to \(p^{-n}\). Dualization computes Ext as the cokernel of

\[
\delta:\prod_{n\ge0}\mathbb Z\longrightarrow\prod_{n\ge0}\mathbb Z,
\qquad\delta(a)_n=a_n-pa_{n+1}.
\]

The convergent sum \(b\mapsto\sum_{n\ge0}p^nb_n\pmod{\mathbb Z}\) induces an isomorphism from that cokernel to \(\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z\). It is onto by p-adic digits; telescoping shows that it kills \(\delta(a)\); and if the sum is an integer \(m\), the integers

\[
a_n=\frac{m-\sum_{k<n}p^kb_k}{p^n}
\]

are well defined and satisfy \(\delta(a)=b\), proving injectivity on the quotient. The quotient is nonzero, indeed uncountable.

Representability of integral cohomology now yields

\[
[T,K(\mathbb Z,2)]\cong\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z,
\qquad
[T_n,K(\mathbb Z,2)]\cong H^2(S^1;\mathbb Z)=0.
\]

Consequently the comparison map is the map from a nontrivial group to a singleton. Every nonzero class gives a nonnull map which is null on every finite subtelescope, and hence on every finite subcomplex: these are phantom maps. This use of unbased homotopy classes is legitimate because the target is simply connected; Hatcher (2002, Theorem 4.57 and the paragraph following it, pp. 393–394) states the unbased identification explicitly.

**Literal presheaf realization of the example.** Set \(X_n=\operatorname{Sing}(T_n)\) and \(Y=\operatorname{Sing}(K(\mathbb Z,2))\). In simplicial sets, the \(X_n\to X_{n+1}\) are monomorphisms/cofibrations and \(Y\) is Kan. The telescope height \(T\to[0,\infty)\) bounds the image of every singular simplex because its domain is compact, so \(\operatorname{Sing}(T)=\bigcup_n\operatorname{Sing}(T_n)\) **as a simplicial set**. Therefore this is exactly a chain of monomorphisms in \(\widehat\Delta\) with ordinary presheaf colimit, as requested at 107/019; no identification of “homotopy colimit” with “ordinary colimit in Hot” has been smuggled in.

### Appendix B. Verification that no ordinary Hot colimit exists

This is our short implication of the checked standard results. Work in the ordinary unbased category of weak homotopy types, equivalently CW homotopy types. Suppose that \(L\) is a colimit of \(S^1\xrightarrow{p}S^1\xrightarrow{p}\cdots\), so that, naturally in every target \(Y\),

\[
[L,Y]\cong\varprojlim_n[S^1,Y].
\]

Testing discrete sets \(D\) gives \(\operatorname{Set}(\pi_0L,D)\cong D\), naturally in \(D\), and therefore \(\pi_0L\) is a singleton by Yoneda. For every abelian group \(A\), functorial Eilenberg–MacLane models give

\[
\operatorname{Hom}(H_1L,A)
\cong H^1(L;A)
\cong[L,K(A,1)]
\cong\varprojlim(A\xleftarrow{p}A\xleftarrow{p}\cdots)
\cong\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z[1/p],A).
\]

These are natural bijections in abelian-group homomorphisms \(A\to A'\), so Yoneda in \(\mathrm{Ab}\) implies \(H_1L\cong\mathbb Z[1/p]\). There is no unbased conjugacy defect here: conjugation is trivial in the abelian target, or one may directly use cohomological representability, including its unbased version (Hatcher, 2002, pp. 393–394). The universal-coefficient theorem, with no finite-generation hypothesis, then injects

\[
0\ne\operatorname{Ext}^1(\mathbb Z[1/p],\mathbb Z)
\cong\widehat{\mathbb Z}_p/\mathbb Z
\hookrightarrow H^2(L;\mathbb Z).
\]

On the other hand, testing \(Y=K(\mathbb Z,2)\) in the presumed universal property forces \(H^2(L;\mathbb Z)=0\), since every \([S^1,K(\mathbb Z,2)]\) is a singleton. This contradiction excludes **every** ordinary colimit object. The finite subtelescope sequence is isomorphic to the degree-\(p\) circle sequence as a diagram in Hot, by terminal-circle deformation retractions and the mapping-cylinder homotopies, so its image in Hot has no ordinary colimit either. A telescope still exists as a homotopy colimit of the coherent diagram.

### Appendix C. Exact rectification hypotheses and proof

#### C1. Simplicial model categories

Fix a strict square and a proposed diagonal:

\[
\begin{array}{ccc}
A&\xrightarrow{g}&E\\
\downarrow i&&\downarrow p\\
B&\xrightarrow{g'}&F,
\end{array}
\qquad pg=g'i,
\qquad\varphi:B\to E.
\]

Assume \(i\) is a cofibration with \(A,B\) cofibrant, \(p\) is a fibration with \(E,F\) fibrant, and the model category is simplicial. SM7 says that

\[
q:\operatorname{Map}(B,E)\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Map}(A,E)\times_{\operatorname{Map}(A,F)}\operatorname{Map}(B,F)
\]

is a Kan fibration. If \(k:\varphi i\simeq g\) and \(h:p\varphi\simeq g'\) satisfy \(pk=h(i\otimes\Delta^1)\), the pair \((k,h)\) is a path in this fibre product from \(q(\varphi)\) to \((g,g')\). Lift this path from \(\varphi\); its endpoint is the required \(\bar\varphi\), satisfying the two equations **strictly** and remaining homotopic to \(\varphi\). Conversely, any homotopy \(H:\varphi\simeq\bar\varphi\) gives the compatible pair \((Hi,pH)\). Thus the existential “if and only if” does not require either \(i\) or \(p\) to be a weak equivalence, and it does not claim uniqueness of the diagonal.

For the weaker formulation in the manuscript margin, suppose that \(pk\) and \(hi\) are homotopic **rel endpoints** in \(\operatorname{Map}(A,F)\). Since \(\operatorname{Map}(A,p)\) is a Kan fibration, the induced map between path spaces with the specified fixed endpoints is a fibration (apply the same pushout-product/pullback-Hom axiom to \(\partial\Delta^1\hookrightarrow\Delta^1\)). Lifting the homotopy of projected paths adjusts \(k\), with endpoints unchanged, to \(\tilde k\) satisfying \(p\tilde k=hi\), after which the preceding argument applies. This is an existence statement about adjustable homotopies; an arbitrary prescribed incompatible pair need not be the restrictions of any homotopy of diagonals.

#### C2. A formulation valid in an arbitrary model category

An ordinary model category need not have a literal simplicial tensor \(B\otimes\Delta^1\); compatible cylinders or framings must be specified. Choose a good cylinder \(CA\) on \(A\), form

\[
Q=(B\amalg B)\amalg_{A\amalg A}CA,
\]

and factor its canonical map to \(B\) as a cofibration \(Q\hookrightarrow CB\) followed by a weak equivalence \(CB\to B\). This gives a compatible cylinder on the cofibration \(i\), and

\[
B\amalg_A CA\longrightarrow CB
\]

is a trivial cofibration: it is a cofibration by construction and pushout stability, while two-out-of-three applies to its composite with \(CB\to B\), using the trivial cofibration \(B\to B\amalg_A CA\). Compatible homotopies \(k:CA\to E\), \(h:CB\to F\), together with \(\varphi\) at the initial endpoint, therefore extend to \(H:CB\to E\) by the lifting axiom against \(p\). This proves the same strict-compatible-data implication without a simplicial enrichment; the cofibrant-source/fibrant-target homotopy comparison supplies the existential converse. To use the margin's higher “homotopic” comparison literally, employ compatible higher cylinders/framings or the derived mapping-space formulation, rather than treating arbitrary cylinders as if they were one common tensor.

#### C3. Why varying both homotopies cannot repair every square

For the square of H07, the two components of a homotopy \(k:\varphi|_{\partial I}\simeq g\) are paths in \(\mathbb R\) from 0 to 0 and from 0 to 1, respectively. Their projections to \(S^1\) have winding numbers 0 and 1, independently of how those paths are chosen. For every \(h:I\times I\to S^1\) from the constant map to itself, its restrictions at the two endpoints of the first interval are homotopic **rel endpoints**, through the first coordinate, so they have the same winding number. Hence no pair \(k,h\) can have equal—or even endpoint-relative homotopic—projections. The fixed strict base map and fixed endpoint map are essential: allowing \(g\) or \(g'\) themselves to change is a different lifting problem.

### Appendix E. Additional verifications for H12–H13

#### E1. Finite presentation of [n] and preservation by the nerve

Let \((C_j)_{j\in J}\) be a filtered diagram of small categories. Its filtered colimit can be described on objects and arrows by the filtered colimits of those sets: source, target, identities, and composition descend because every finite collection of representatives and every finite equation can be placed at a common stage. For a functor \([n]\to\operatorname{colim}_j C_j\), choose representatives of its n generating arrows and n+1 objects, then move to a common stage where the required source and target equalities hold. Their composites define a functor \([n]\to C_j\), proving surjectivity of

\[
\operatorname{colim}_j\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([n],C_j)
\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}}([n],\operatorname{colim}_jC_j).
\]

If two representatives define the same functor in the colimit, their finitely many object and generating-arrow equalities hold at a common later stage, proving injectivity. This bijection is natural in [n]; it therefore gives the asserted isomorphism of simplicial sets. This is our elementary verification of the reason already supplied in the manuscript and restated in the companion commentary at V ms. 46, not a further external theorem or historical-priority claim.

#### E2. A sequence of retracts with no retraction from its union

Use the literal simplicial example of Appendix A, and choose a simplicial representative \(f:X_\infty\to Y\) of a nonzero class, with \(Y=\operatorname{Sing}K(\mathbb Z,2)\) Kan and zero map \(c:X_\infty\to Y\). Every restriction \(f_i\) is simplicially homotopic to \(c_i\), since all simplicial sets are cofibrant and the target is Kan. Form the pushout

\[
Z_i=Y\amalg_{X_i\times\partial\Delta^1}(X_i\times\Delta^1),
\]

where the boundary map is \((f_i,c_i)\). The inclusion \(Y\to Z_i\) is a monomorphism. A retraction \(Z_i\to Y\) restricting to \(\mathrm{id}_Y\) is, by the pushout universal property, exactly a simplicial homotopy from \(f_i\) to \(c_i\); hence every stage admits such a retraction. The transition \(Z_i\to Z_{i+1}\) is the pushout of the monomorphism

\[
(X_i\times\Delta^1)\cup(X_{i+1}\times\partial\Delta^1)
\hookrightarrow X_{i+1}\times\Delta^1,
\]

so it too is a monomorphism. Colimits and the pushout formula give

\[
Z_\infty=Y\amalg_{X_\infty\times\partial\Delta^1}
(X_\infty\times\Delta^1).
\]

A retraction of this object onto Y would be a homotopy \(f\simeq c\), contrary to the chosen nonzero class; thus there is no global retraction.

For completeness, let \(R_i\) be the simplicial mapping space of retractions under Y, equivalently the path space from \(f_i\) to \(c_i\) in \(\operatorname{Map}(X_i,Y)\). These are nonempty Kan complexes, and each restriction \(R_{i+1}\to R_i\) is a Kan fibration by the displayed monomorphism and the mapping-space axiom. The ordinary limit \(\lim_iR_i\) is precisely the retraction space for \(Z_\infty\). If a compatible sequence of components existed, choose a representative at stage zero and, inductively, lift a path from a later representative’s restriction to the already chosen earlier representative; this produces strictly compatible representatives. It follows that \(\lim_i\pi_0R_i\) is empty here: the manuscript’s final obstruction persists even for a genuine tower of retraction spaces with all the stated cofibration and fibrancy properties.

Consulted the publisher's Chapter IX abstract and its original two-page preview, **Chapter IX, §1, pp. 249–250**, which explicitly announces the extension of the tower exact sequence to \(\pi_0\) and nonabelian \(\lim^1\): https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-540-38117-4_9 and https://page-one.springer.com/pdf/preview/10.1007/978-3-540-38117-4_9 . The location **IX.3.1** is corroborated by Hirschhorn's introductory attribution, but the original full §3 was **not** independently inspected; the fully checked theorem used here is Hirschhorn's Theorem 2.1.

Consulted **Theorem 3.2, p. 195** (universal coefficients, with no finite-generation restriction); **§3F, Example 3F.3, p. 312; Example 3F.7, p. 314; Theorem 3F.8, pp. 314–315; Example 3F.9, p. 315; the subsequent Ext calculation, p. 316**; and **Theorem 4.57 with the unbased clarification, pp. 393–394**. These locations are internal printed pages in the author PDF; the corresponding zero-based PDF indexes for pp. 314–316 are 322–324. The telescope counterexample is already explicit in this reference.

**Hirschhorn, P. S. (2015, May 16).** *The homotopy groups of the inverse limit of a tower of fibrations* [Author's expository note]. https://math.mit.edu/~psh/notes/limfibrations.pdf

Consulted **Definition 1.1, p. 1**, for the product-group action defining nonabelian \(\lim^1\), and **Theorem 2.1, pp. 1–2**, including its pointed-set qualification at degree zero; inspected the explanatory proof in **§4**. The note identifies earlier proofs and the original Bousfield–Kan location, so it is not used to suggest that the tower theorem originated in 2015.

**Hovey, M. (1999).** *Model categories* (Mathematical Surveys and Monographs, Vol. 63). American Mathematical Society. https://doi.org/10.1090/surv/063

Consulted the 213-page author-text PDF hosted by Douglas Ravenel: https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/hovey-model-cats.pdf . Exact locations **in that consulted version** are **Definition 4.2.1 and Lemma 4.2.2, internal pp. 107–108**, for pushout-product/pullback-Hom, and **Definition 4.2.18, pp. 114–115**, for simplicial model categories; **Proposition 5.4.3, p. 130**, supplies the analogous trivial-cofibration property for compatible frames. Pagination is deliberately version-specific rather than silently assumed to coincide with every printing.

**Lurie, J. (2026).** *Kerodon*, Proposition 3.2.8.3 (Tag 00XE). Retrieved November 16, 2026, from https://kerodon.net/tag/00XE

Consulted the full statement and proof, which assert closure of weak homotopy equivalences of simplicial sets under filtered colimits. The author and bibliographic form are confirmed by the site's own citation page, https://kerodon.net/tag/00XE/cite ; the proposition is used only for the scoped claim A = Δ, W∞ in H11, not for an unspecified localizer.

**Milnor, J. (1962).** On axiomatic homology theory. *Pacific Journal of Mathematics, 12*(1), 337–341. Publisher's original PDF: https://msp.org/pjm/1962/12-1/pjm-v12-n1-p31-p.pdf

Consulted **Lemma 1, p. 337; definition of the derived inverse limit and Lemma 2, p. 338; proofs, pp. 338–339**. Lemma 2 is the precise primary source for surjectivity of the cohomological comparison and its \(\lim^1 H^{n-1}\) kernel for an increasing CW-subcomplex exhaustion. No quotation longer than a brief identifying phrase is needed.

The exact supplied statement text, status, and source locator appear in both this table and the companion JSON for audit.

- **Direct partial form** means an identifiable restricted manuscript construction intersects the claimed statement; it does not assert the full modern result.
- **Thematic link** means relevant objects or questions occur, but the supplied claim is not stated or proved in that form.
- **Absent** is bounded to these 41 frozen scan blocks, with the specific missing scope explained in each note; it is not an archive-wide absence claim.
- All `claim (return: …)` strings are copied exactly from `context/DOSSIER_STATEMENTS_WITH_STATUS.md`; they are the supplied run’s status, not this note’s verdict on mathematical truth.
- Every row says whether its own ID is selected in RESULTS_STATE. Only **PATHS D.22** is selected among these 86 IDs; related selected entries are identified with their exact status, source file, and locator.

#### Principal findings

1. The closest match is PATHS A.P1, and even that is restricted to a revised short-fraction construction: 106 claims surjectivity, flags faithfulness as unclear, and ends with a conditional section criterion.
2. None of the ten STRATA claims occurs in its stated form. The lone thematic link is G.2 through the separate filtered-colimit question in Hot.
3. 107 distinguishes compatibility of chosen homotopies from merely having each triangle commute up to homotopy; this is relevant to PATHS rectification themes but is not a theorem of diagram-category rectification for all shapes.
4. The source explicitly preserves the unresolved injectivity of the sequential Hom-set map. This cannot be used as a proof of either total asphericity or a Cat-valued colimit comparison.
5. No first historical appearance of strong W(7) or total-aspherical ⇒ filtered is established by these cotes. The precise supplied later-text leads are XII ms.75–77 and II ms.36/36ter, and their companion/modern-review provenance remains visible.
6. PATHS C.P3 and selected CATHOT E.26 have different supplied claim statuses; the package does not resolve that register discrepancy.

No source attachments were copied into this working record, no scans were viewed by this agent, and no transcription files were edited. No external mathematical claims are added beyond the explicitly attributed supplied materials.

### J.2 BISITE and RESIDUE: 169 statements

This research mapping uses the frozen transcription of 106/002–017 and 107/002–026, after reading `context/README.md` and `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`. The supplied register labels are claims of the recorded runs; the selected status file records no referee or intake review of BISITE or RESIDUE. Absence here means absence of a formulation in these folders, not a mathematical refutation of the registered statement.

**Coverage:** 169 rows, comprising 113 BISITE and 56 RESIDUE; 0 direct formulations, 5 partial underived precursors, 15 thematic links, and 149 absent formulations. Exactly 14 row IDs occur in the selected `RESULTS_STATE`; 155 do not. The source transcription SHA-256 remains `516a428d7992190dbc5aa630f0c065214ccb54909e6633801e272ffeeb9237f9`.

**Classification:** “Direct formulation” would require the registered assertion itself; none was found. “Partial underived precursor” identifies an ordinary construction with a precise formal connection, without a claim of chronology or derivation of the modern theorem. “Thematic link” identifies a related question while keeping the registered assertion absent. “Absent” gives no matching formulation; unrelated appearances of words such as Hom, projection, product, dual, span, or limit do not count as an occurrence.

##### BISITE

| ID and subject | Full supplied statement | Supplied status (exact) | Supplied locator (exact) | Comparison, scan, and words | Selected RESULTS_STATE |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BISITE A.1 — literal saturation axioms and the distinction between absolute and relative Theorem A (dossier line 115) | The historical and adopted localizer axioms are different formulations. The literal Loc~(1) of XVI ms.~1 consists of isomorphisms, two-out-of-three, and the condition that $gf,fg\in W$ imply $f,g\in W$. Grothendieck calls the first two conditions \emph{saturation faible}, and all three \emph{saturation mod\'er\'ee}. Retract closure is added in Loc~(1 bis); strong saturation is Loc~(1 ter). The literal Loc~(3), XVI ms.~2, is absolute Theorem~A. Relative Theorem~A is Loc~(3 bis), ms.~3. In this paper, a fundamental localizer means the modern relative package specified in the conventions: weak saturation, the terminal-object axiom, and the assertion that a functor $u:A\to B$ over $C$ lies in $W$ whenever $u/c:A/c\to B/c$ lies in $W$ for every $c\in C$. Consequently no argument using relative Theorem~A is an argument from the literal absolute Loc~(3) alone. | claim (return: Proved) | XVI ms.1--3 | **Absent.** Absent: no formulation of literal saturation axioms and the distinction between absolute and relative Theorem A occurs in the frozen 106/002–017 and 107/002–026 corpus. | Absent from the selected RESULTS_STATE. |
| BISITE A.2 — intrinsic fibrations defined by universal pullback preservation and their closure properties (dossier line 116) | Intrinsic fibrations and their unconditional closure properties. Let $\mathcal M$ have pullbacks and let $W$ be invariant under isomorphism of arrows. For $S\in\mathcal M$, let $W_S$ be the inverse image of $W$ under the source functor $\mathcal M/S\to\mathcal M$. Define \[ \operatorname{Fib}_W=\{p:E\to B\mid p^*: \mathcal M/B\to\mathcal M/E\text{ sends }W_B\text{ into }W_E\}. \] Thus every test is an arrow $v:T'\to T$ over $B$, with arbitrary structure map $T\to B$, and the tested arrow is $E\times_B T'\to E\times_B T$. This class contains isomorphisms and is closed under composition and arbitrary pullback. These assertions require no two-out-of-three axiom. If $W$ is closed under retracts, then so is $\operatorname{Fib}_W$. | claim (return: Proved) | XII ms.207--209; GALOIS D.1 | **Absent.** Absent: no formulation of intrinsic fibrations defined by universal pullback preservation and their closure properties occurs in the frozen 106/002–017 and 107/002–026 corpus. | Absent from the selected RESULTS_STATE. |
| BISITE A.3 — the coarse, universal, and locally tested weak-equivalence classes on slices (dossier line 117) | The coarse, universal, and local relative classes. Suppose $W$ contains isomorphisms and satisfies two-out-of-three. Let $\mathcal L$ contain isomorphisms and be closed under composition and pullback. For an arrow $u:X\to Y$ in $\mathcal M/S$, put \[ u\in W_S^{\mathcal L}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \bigl(X\times_S T\longrightarrow Y\times_S T\bigr)\in W \quad\hbox{for every }(T\to S)\in\mathcal L. \] Let $W_S^u$ be the same class with all $T\to S$ allowed. Then \[ W_S^u\subseteq W_S^{\mathcal L}\subseteq W_S. \] All three classes contain isomorphisms and satisfy two-out-of-three. Their retract closure follows if $W$ has it. These are precisely the relative classes in XII ms.~210 and 220. They must not be confused with the class of universal $W$-equivalences in $\mathcal M$, where the base is the codomain of the arrow being tested. | claim (return: Proved) | XII ms.210,220; GALOIS A.5 | **Absent.** Absent: no formulation of the coarse, universal, and locally tested weak-equivalence classes on slices occurs in the frozen 106/002–017 and 107/002–026 corpus. | Absent from the selected RESULTS_STATE. |
| BISITE A.4 — descent of postcomposition and restricted raw reindexing to fine localizations (dossier line 118) | What raw reindexing and postcomposition actually preserve. Under the hypotheses of A.3, a map $f:S'\to S$ in $\mathcal L$ induces \[ f^*:(\mathcal M/S,W_S^{\mathcal L})\longrightarrow (\mathcal M/S',W_{S'}^{\mathcal L}). \] For every $f:S'\to S$, without assuming $f\in\mathcal L$, postcomposition induces \[ f_\#:(\mathcal M/S',W_{S'}^{\mathcal L})\longrightarrow (\mathcal M/S,W_S^{\mathcal L}). \] If $f^*$ also preserves the displayed classes, the ordinary adjunction $f_\#\dashv f^*$ descends to their localizations. For arbitrary $f$, this calculation alone constructs the descended postcomposition functor; it does not construct its right adjoint. | claim (return: Proved) | XII ms.226--227,248 | **Absent.** Absent: no formulation of descent of postcomposition and restricted raw reindexing to fine localizations occurs in the frozen 106/002–017 and 107/002–026 corpus. | Absent from the selected RESULTS_STATE. |
| BISITE A.5 — universal saturation of the local test class and its maximality (dossier line 119) | The universal saturation of the local test class. Under A.3, define $\widehat{\mathcal L}$ by the following condition on $f:S'\to S$: for every $T\to S$, pullback along $f_T:T\times_S S'\to T$ sends $W_T^{\mathcal L}$ into $W_{T\times_S S'}^{\mathcal L}$. | RESIDUE D.12 — trivial monodromy characterizing integral and rational intrinsic homology coverings (dossier line 271) | Trivial monodromy is necessary and sufficient for integral and rational homology. Let $p:E\to B$ be a categorical covering, that is, a discrete fibration and discrete opfibration. For either $k=\mathbb Z$ or $k=\mathbb Q$, it belongs to $\Fib_{W_{Hk}}$ if and only if its monodromy on every fibre set is trivial. Equivalently, over each connected component $B_0$ it is isomorphic to a product $B_0\times S_0\to B_0$, where $S_0$ may be empty or infinite. In particular a nonempty connected covering of a connected base is intrinsic for either of these two homology localizers exactly when it is isomorphic over the base to its identity covering. ## K. Completion, validation limits, and recovery records

The package contains all **41 page fragments**, in the requested order (106/002–017, then 107/002–026), and their combined preamble-free fragment. The inventory has **1,202 rows**, each marked `yes`; the doubts register has **342 entries**, preceded by the recurring-notation table and followed by abbreviation and legibility information. All 41 scans received a separate visual audit before the freeze. The scans, attached archives, and supplied context are excluded from the delivered ZIP.

The final integrity check compares all **42 SHA-256 values** (41 page fragments and the combined transcription) with the record made at the **2026-11-16 13:43:19 UTC** freeze. The final check result and its UTC time are recorded in `progress.log` and `records/final_validation.json`. The context was opened only after the freeze; post-freeze changes concern research notes and their supporting records.

**Typesetting limitation:** structural validation found balanced braces, environments, and math delimiters in all page fragments, with the combined text matching the ordered fragments. A full LaTeX compilation could not be performed: the available XeTeX executable lacked `xelatex.fmt`, `latex.ltx`, `tikz-cd.sty`, and `fontspec.sty`. The attempted runs and the environment failure are retained in `records/latex_validation.json`; no successful compilation or rendered-page verification is claimed. The requested deliverable remains a LaTeX fragment with the user-specified state macros, without a preamble.

An independent research-only validation compared the two dossier JSON records against all **255** source rows: every ID, statement, supplied status, locator, and recorded source line matched, with no omissions or duplicates. It also checked the **12** numbered NEW_RESULTS entries, all **60** non-ID metadata cells, and the **3** concluding notices. The dossier comparison classifications are **212 absent**, **37 thematic links**, **1 direct partial form**, and **5 partial underived precursors**; none is a full attestation of the complete supplied modern statement. The supplied statuses remain **146 Proved**, **33 Known**, **57 Refuted**, and **19 Open**, each explicitly a supplied return claim rather than a new verdict in this report.

The supporting `records/` and `scripts/` directories are included for provenance and recovery. The historical `page_*.py` scripts contain the first-pass page texts; **do not rerun them over the frozen files**, because the later visual corrections are recorded in `audit_changes.diff` and already incorporated in `pages/`. `manuscript_leads.md` is likewise a first-pass working record, superseded where the audit or final notes correct it. `frozen_sha256.json`, `visual_audit.md`, `notation_audit.md`, and `structural_validation.json` record the freeze boundary; the research JSONs preserve exact concordance data. `context_search_root.md` and its JSON preserve bounded search locators without redistributing the supplied source files.

The manuscript's unresolved claims, the supplied registers' statuses, and our explicitly scoped mathematical verifications remain separate. Neither a successful modern proof nor a contradictory contextual parallel has changed a letter of the frozen transcription.
